I don't think so. I haven't really had any strange ideas or anything like that. Nobody at work or anywhere else has ever said I believe things that aren't true. I just think about normal stuff, I guess.\n\nDoctor: When you freeze like that, are you aware of what is happening around you?\n\nPatient: I... I'm not really sure. I don't think so. It's just kind of a blank, I guess. I don't really remember what's going on or what the other guys are doing until I finally snap out of it.\n\nDoctor: Do these freezing episodes happen when you’re under a lot of stress or strong emotions?\n\nPatient: Um, I'm not really sure. I haven't really noticed if it's tied to being stressed out or anything like that. I mean, the warehouse gets busy and all, but... I don't think I'm feeling any strong emotions before it happens. It just sort of comes out of nowhere, honestly. I haven't really seen a pattern with it.\n\nDoctor: Have other people told you that your speech is hard to follow or that your answers don’t make sense?\n\nPatient: Yeah, they have. Like, at work, when the supervisor asks me which pallets are going on the truck, I start trying to explain it to him. But... my words just kind of jump around. I'll start talking about the load sheet, and then somehow I'm talking about my old house, and then fishing, and then church boxes. I don't really realize I'm doing it, but by the time I'm done talking, nobody knows what I meant. So, yeah, it gets hard for them to follow.\n\nDoctor: When you freeze like that, does your body feel stiff or tense, as if your muscles are locked up?\n\nPatient: I don't really know. Like I said, it's just a blank. I'm not feeling my muscles or noticing if I'm stiff or anything. I just kind of... stop. And then when I come back, I don't really remember how my body felt while it was happening. So, no, I haven't noticed feeling locked up.\n\nDoctor: When you freeze like that, are you completely unable to move your body, or can you move if you try?\n\nPatient: I'm really not sure if I could move or not. Like I was saying, it's just a blank space for me. I don't remember trying to move, so I don't know if I'm completely stuck or if I could snap out of it if I really tried. I just don't have any memory of what's going on with my body right then.\n\nDoctor: When someone tries to move your arms or legs during one of these freezing episodes, do they stay in whatever position they’re placed in?\n\nPatient: I honestly don't know. Nobody's ever told me they tried to move my arms or legs while I was blanked out like that. Like I said, I don't remember any of it, so... I just don't have an answer for that.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever believed that other people were watching you, talking about you, or trying to harm you?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't ever thought anything like that. I mean, nobody's watching me or talking about me that I know of. And I definitely don't think anyone is trying to hurt me. I just... I don't really have any worries like that.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever been told you have a neurological or medical illness, like seizures, a brain injury, or an infection, that could explain these episodes?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't. Nobody has ever told me I have anything like that. I've never had a seizure or a brain injury or... any kind of infection like you're talking about. As far as I know, there's nothing physically wrong with me like that.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. Nobody's ever told me they tried to move my arms or legs while I was blanked out like that. Like I said, I don't remember any of it, so... I just don't have an answer for that.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever believed that other people were watching you, talking about you, or trying to harm you?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't ever thought anything like that. I mean, nobody's watching me or talking about me that I know of. And I definitely don't think anyone is trying to hurt me. I just... I don't really have any worries like that.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever been told you have a neurological or medical illness, like seizures, a brain injury, or an infection, that could explain these episodes?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't. Nobody has ever told me I have anything like that. I've never had a seizure or a brain injury or... any kind of infection like you're talking about. As far as I know, there's nothing physically wrong with me like that.\n\nDoctor: During one of those freezing episodes, can you hear and understand people speaking to you?\n\nPatient: I really couldn't tell you. I haven't really noticed if it's tied to being stressed out or anything like that. I mean, the warehouse gets busy and all, but... I don't think I'm feeling any strong emotions before it happens. It just sort of comes out of nowhere, honestly. I haven't really seen a pattern with it.\n\nDoctor: Have other people told you that your speech is hard to follow or that your answers don’t make sense?\n\nPatient: Yeah, they have. Like, at work, when the supervisor asks me which pallets are going on the truck, I start trying to explain it to him. But... my words just kind of jump around. I'll start talking about the load sheet, and then somehow I'm talking about my old house, and then fishing, and then church boxes. I don't really realize I'm doing it, but by the time I'm done talking, nobody knows what I meant. So, yeah, it gets hard for them to follow.\n\nDoctor: When you freeze like that, does your body feel stiff or tense, as if your muscles are locked up?\n\nPatient: I don't really know. Like I said, it's just a blank. I'm not feeling my muscles or noticing if I'm stiff or anything. I just kind of... stop. And then when I come back, I don't really remember how my body felt while it was happening. So, no, I haven't noticed feeling locked up.\n\nDoctor: When you freeze like that, are you completely unable to move your body, or can you move if you try?\n\nPatient: I'm really not sure if I could move or not. Like I was saying, it's just a blank space for me. I don't remember trying to move, so I don't know if I'm completely stuck or if I could snap out of it if I really tried. I just don't have any memory of what's going on with my body right then.\n\nDoctor: When someone tries to move your arms or legs during one of these freezing episodes, do they stay in whatever position they’re placed in?\n\nPatient: I honestly don't know. Nobody's ever told me they tried to move my arms or legs while I was blanked out like that. Like I said, I don't remember any of it, so... I just don't have an answer for that.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever believed that other people were watching you, talking about you, or trying to harm you?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't ever thought anything like that. I mean, nobody's watching me or talking about me that I know of. And I definitely don't think anyone is trying to hurt me. I just... I don't really have any worries like that.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever been told you have a neurological or medical illness, like seizures, a brain injury, or an infection, that could explain these episodes?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't. Nobody has ever told me I have anything like that. I've never had a seizure or a brain injury or... any kind of infection like you're talking about. As far as I know, there's nothing physically wrong with me like that.\n\nDoctor: During one of those freezing episodes, can you hear and understand people speaking to you?\n\nPatient: I really couldn't tell you. Like I mentioned before, I don't remember anything when those happen. So if someone is talking to me, I don't hear them... or at least, I have absolutely no memory of hearing or understanding them. It's just a total blank for me.\n\nDoctor: During one of the freezing episodes, are you aware of what is happening around you?\n\nPatient: No, I'm not. It's kind of like what I was just saying about not hearing people... I don't have any idea what's going on around me when it happens. I'm just completely out of it, I guess. So, no, I don't have any awareness at all.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever heard voices when no one was there?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't. I mean, I don't hear people talking when nobody is around, if that's what you mean. It's just... normal stuff. No voices like that.\n\nDoctor: During those freezing episodes, do you ever hold a fixed posture, like your arms staying up in one position, for a long time?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I guess that's actually why they brought me in here. At the warehouse where I work, I keep doing things like that... just freezing with my arms up by the forklift for a long time. And I guess I also copy the beeping sounds and the hand signals, or I'll start moving boxes around in these strange little patterns. I just keep doing it even when everyone is yelling for me to stop.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out.
